\end{proof}

We next isolate the precise base-independent interface. This is
deliberately narrower than every object called a Pisot or abstract
numeration system in the literature.

\begin{definition}[Canonical eventually recurrent numeration]
  \label{def:canonical-eventually-recurrent-numeration}
  A canonical eventually recurrent numeration system consists of:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(U\arabic*)}]
    \item a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
    \(U=(U_n)_{n\ge0}\) and a finite digit alphabet
    \(A\subseteq\NN\) containing \(0\);
    \item an LSD-first language \(\mathcal R_U\subseteq A^\ast\), with
    \[
      \val_U(x_0\cdots x_{m-1})
        :=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_jU_j,
    \]
    for which \(\val_U:\mathcal R_U\to\NN_{\ge1}\) is a bijection;
    \item strict length ordering: if \(r,s\in\mathcal R_U\) and
    \(\abs r<\abs s\), then \(\val_U(r)<\val_U(s)\);
    \item integers \(J\ge0,d\ge1,a_0,\ldots,a_{d-1}\), with
    \(a_0\ne0\), such that
    \[
      U_{n+d}=a_0U_n+a_1U_{n+1}+\cdots+a_{d-1}U_{n+d-1}
      \qquad(n\ge J).
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
  Relative to this recurrence witness, it is a \emph{unit system} if
  \(\abs{a_0}=1\). No regularity of \(\mathcal R_U\) is included in
  this definition.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Affine recurrence block action]
  \label{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}
  Under \textup{(U4)}, let \(C\) be the \(d\times d\) companion matrix
  with superdiagonal entries \(1\) and last row
  \((a_0,\ldots,a_{d-1})\). For \(\varepsilon\in A\), put
  \[
    D_\varepsilon=
    \begin{pmatrix}
      1&\varepsilon e_1^t\\
      0&C
    \end{pmatrix}.
  \]
  Once \(r\ge J\), appending \(\varepsilon\) sends
  \[
    (N_r,U_r,\ldots,U_{r+d-1})^t
    \quad\hbox{to}\quad
    D_\varepsilon(N_r,U_r,\ldots,U_{r+d-1})^t.
  \]
  Moreover
  \(\det D_\varepsilon=(-1)^{d-1}a_0\). Hence every block action is
  invertible modulo \(q\) whenever \(\gcd(q,a_0)=1\), and modulo every
  \(q\ge2\) in a unit system. Here and below, for a block
  \(b=b_1\cdots b_\ell\), we write
  \(M_b=D_{b_\ell}\cdots D_{b_1}\), with \(M_\varepsilon=I\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  The first coordinate becomes \(N_r+\varepsilon U_r\); the remaining
  coordinates shift once and the final one is supplied by \textup{(U4)}.
  These are exactly the displayed matrix equations. The determinant
  is \(\det C=(-1)^{d-1}a_0\). Multiplicativity of determinants gives
  the assertion for blocks.
\end{proof}

The coefficient \(a_0\) belongs to the displayed recurrence witness,
not intrinsically to \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\); see
\Cref{prop:recurrence-witness-separation} for the resulting strict
separation.

We now use the standard fan-out convention: a multiple context-free
language (MCFL) is a \(k\)-MCFL for some finite \(k\ge1\). An infinite
language is \emph{MCF-immune} if it contains no infinite MCFL
sublanguage. The weak pumping lemma of Seki--Matsumura--Fujii--Kasami
states that an infinite \(k\)-MCFL contains one word that can be pumped
synchronously at \(2k\) factors \cite[Lemma~3.2]{SekiEtAl1991}. The
  lemma makes no assertion about every sufficiently long word; that
  stronger form fails for general MCFLs
  \cite{KanazawaKobeleMichaelisSalvatiYoshinaka2014}.

  Duncan--Elder--Frenkel--Lyu's substitution lemma
  \cite[Theorem~4.5]{DuncanElderFrenkelLiu2026} is stronger in a different
  quantifier direction: after enough positions of each sufficiently long
  word are marked, it gives either a bounded pump or a factorization in
  which a tuple can be replaced by another tuple from one of finitely many
  switchable families. It does not select a single family parameterized by
  one exponent \(t\), nor does it make all replacement blocks simultaneous
  powers of fixed words. Consequently it supplies neither a fixed affine
  matrix-power expression nor a return time valid for every parameter on
  one orbit. The switchable alternative also prevents it from replacing the
  paired Ogden pump in the Zeckendorf argument above. Thus it does not by
  itself imply any immunity result proved here. We use Seki et al.'s weaker
  conclusion precisely because its one synchronized family has the fixed
  \(2k\)-block power form needed below. The imported language statement is
  only that family; its placement beyond the recurrence transient, the
  finite-group return, and all arithmetic consequences are proved here.

\begin{theorem}[Synchronized local-congruence orbit]
  \label{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}
  Let \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\) satisfy \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, and let
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) be an infinite \(k\)-MCFL. There are words
  \[
    \begin{gathered}
      a,u_1,\ldots,u_{k+1},v_1,\ldots,v_k\in A^\ast,\\
      w_1,\ldots,w_k,s_1,\ldots,s_k\in A^\ast
    \end{gathered}
  \]
  such that
  \[
    W(t)=a u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^tu_2\cdots
          u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}\in L
          \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  \[
    \sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0,
  \]
  and \(N(t):=\val_U(W(t))\) is strictly increasing. Moreover, for
  every \(q\ge2\) with \(\gcd(q,a_0)=1\), there is \(H(q)\ge1\) such
  that
  \[
    N(t+H(q))\equiv N(t)\pmod q
    \qquad\text{for every }t\ge0.
  \]
  The factorization, and hence the orbit \(W(t)\), is independent of
  \(q\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Delete the finitely many words of length less than \(J\). Since the
  alphabet is finite, some \(a\in A^J\) is the prefix of infinitely many
  remaining words; when \(J=0\), take \(a=\varepsilon\). For completeness,
  fixed left quotient preserves fan-out. Introduce a fresh letter \(\#\),
  let \(h(\#)=a\) and \(h(x)=x\) for \(x\in A\), intersect
  \(h^{-1}(L)\) with the regular language \(\#A^*\), and erase \(\#\).
  Inverse homomorphism, regular intersection, and homomorphism preserve
  \(k\)-MCFLs without increasing the maximum nonterminal dimension
  \cite[Proposition~2.3]{DuncanElderFrenkelLiu2026}. Hence
  \[
    L_a=\{x\in A^\ast:ax\in L\}
  \]
  is an infinite \(k\)-MCFL. The weak pumping lemma supplies
  \(u_j,v_j,w_j,s_j\) with the displayed synchronized pumping property
  and with at least one \(v_j\) or \(s_j\) nonempty.

  All digit actions after \(a\) are in the stationary range of
  \textup{(U4)}. Fix an allowed modulus \(q\). By
  \Cref{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}, every nonempty matrix among
  \[
    M_{v_1},M_{s_1},\ldots,M_{v_k},M_{s_k}
  \]
  lies in the finite group
  \(\operatorname{GL}_{d+1}(\ZZ/q\ZZ)\). One may take
  \[
    H(q)=\abs{\operatorname{GL}_{d+1}(\ZZ/q\ZZ)}
      \le q^{(d+1)^2};
  \]
  Lagrange's theorem makes this a common multiple of their orders, and
  empty blocks contribute the identity.
  Replacing \(t\) by \(t+H(q)\) in the matrix product for \(W(t)\)
  therefore leaves every powered block unchanged modulo \(q\), proving
  the congruence simultaneously for all \(t\ge0\).

  Finally,
  \[
    \abs{W(t+1)}-\abs{W(t)}
      =\sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0.
  \]
  Both words are canonical, so \textup{(U3)} gives
  \(N(t+1)>N(t)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Explicit grammar for a synchronized ray]
  \label{lem:synchronized-ray-linear-mcfg}
  Fix \(k\ge1\), a word \(a\), and words
  \(u_1,\ldots,u_{k+1},v_1,\ldots,v_k,w_1,\ldots,w_k,
  s_1,\ldots,s_k\) over a finite alphabet. If
  \[
    \sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0,
  \]
  then
  \[
    \left\{a u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^tu_2\cdots
      u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}:t\ge0\right\}
  \]
  is an infinite linear \(2k\)-MCFL.
\end{lemma}


 succeeded in 278ms:
\cite{Broughan2003}. His Theorem~2.4 and Corollary~3.1 supply the
\(\mathbb Q\)-space and Cantor-completion classifications used below. The
new assertion here is that every synchronized MCFL orbit satisfies their
no-isolated-point hypothesis, and hence yields the scattered-set
obstruction.

\begin{theorem}[Adic recurrence and scattered-set obstruction for synchronized MCFL orbits]
  \label{thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint}
  For the orbit \(Y=\{N(t):t\ge0\}\) supplied by
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, the following hold.
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item The subspace \(Y\subseteq(\NN_{\ge1},\tau_S)\) has no isolated
    points and is homeomorphic to \(\mathbb Q\).
    \item Its closure in \(\widehat{\ZZ}_{S^c}\) is homeomorphic to the
    Cantor set.
    \item If \(B\subseteq\NN_{\ge1}\) is scattered in \(\tau_S\), then
    \[
      \operatorname{Rep}_U(B)
        :=\{w\in\mathcal R_U:\val_U(w)\in B\}
    \]
    is MCF-immune whenever it is infinite.
  \end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Let \(N(t)\in Y\), and let its basic neighborhood be specified modulo
  an allowed \(q\). If \(q=1\), take any later orbit point. If \(q\ge2\),
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit} gives
  \(N(t+H(q))\equiv N(t)\pmod q\), and strict increase makes these points
  distinct. Thus \(Y\) has no isolated point.

  The allowed congruences separate distinct positive integers: for
  \(m\ne n\), choose a prime outside the finite set \(S\) that does not
  divide \(m-n\). Consequently \(\tau_S\) is Hausdorff and metrizable;
  it also has a countable basis. Hence \(Y\) is a countable metrizable
  space without isolated points. The Sierpi\'nski characterization used
  in \cite[Theorem~2.4]{Broughan2003} yields \(Y\cong\mathbb Q\).

  The profinite ring \(\widehat{\ZZ}_{S^c}\) is compact, metrizable, and
  zero-dimensional. Its closed subspace \(\overline Y\) has no isolated
  point: an isolated point of the closure would, by density of \(Y\),
  belong to and be isolated in \(Y\). Thus \(\overline Y\) is a nonempty
  compact perfect zero-dimensional metrizable space, and the classical
  Cantor-space characterization gives the second assertion; compare
  \cite[Corollary~3.1]{Broughan2003}.

  For the last assertion, an infinite MCFL sublanguage of
  \(\operatorname{Rep}_U(B)\) would, by the first assertion, provide a
  nonempty subspace \(Y\subseteq B\) without isolated points. This
  contradicts scatteredness, which is hereditary to subspaces.
\end{proof}

For a prime \(p\), write \(v_p(n)\) for the exponent of \(p\) in \(n\),
and let \(\omega_{S^c}(n)=\#\{p\notin S:p\mid n\}\).
For \(K\ge0\) and \(E=(E_p)_{p\in S}\in\NN_0^S\), define
\[
  X_{K,E}=\{n\ge1:\omega_{S^c}(n)\le K,
               \ v_p(n)\le E_p\ (p\in S)\}.
\]
For \(X\subseteq\NN_{\ge1}\), let \(D_SX\) be the set of points of \(X\)
that are not isolated in its \(\tau_S\)-subspace topology. Thus \(D_S\)
is the intrinsic Cantor--Bendixson derivative. We use the height convention
\[
  \operatorname{ht}(X)=\min\{\alpha:D_S^\alpha X=\varnothing\}.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Exact local prime-factor rank]
  \label{prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank}
  For every \(K\ge1\),
  \[
    D_SX_{K,E}=X_{K-1,E}.
  \]
  Consequently
  \[
    D_S^{\,j}X_{K,E}=X_{K-j,E}\quad(0\le j\le K),
    \qquad D_S^{\,K+1}X_{K,E}=\varnothing,
  \]
  and the Cantor--Bendixson height of \(X_{K,E}\) is exactly \(K+1\).
  For \(K=0\), the nonempty set \(X_{0,E}\) is finite,
  \(D_SX_{0,E}=\varnothing\), and its height is \(1\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose first that \(n\in X_{K-1,E}\), and let \(q\ge1\) be coprime
  to \(a_0\). Dirichlet's theorem supplies a prime
  \(r\equiv1\pmod q\) outside the finite set \(S\cup\{p:p\mid n\}\).
  Then \(nr\ne n\), \(nr\equiv n\pmod q\), the \(S\)-adic valuations
  are unchanged, and
  \[
    \omega_{S^c}(nr)=\omega_{S^c}(n)+1\le K.
  \]
  Hence \(n\in D_SX_{K,E}\).

  Conversely, let \(n\in X_{K,E}\) have
  \(\omega_{S^c}(n)=K\), and write
  \[
    n=c\prod_{i=1}^K\ell_i^{e_i},
    \qquad c=\prod_{p\in S}p^{f_p},\quad 0\le f_p\le E_p,
  \]
  with distinct \(\ell_i\notin S\). Put
  \(q_0=\prod_i\ell_i^{e_i+1}\). If \(m\in X_{K,E}\) and
  \(m\equiv n\pmod {q_0}\), then
  \(v_{\ell_i}(m)=e_i\) for every \(i\). The budget of \(K\) primes
  outside \(S\) leaves no further such prime, so \(m\) belongs to the
  finite set
  \[
    F=\left\{
      \left(\prod_{p\in S}p^{g_p}\right)
      \prod_{i=1}^K\ell_i^{e_i}:
      0\le g_p\le E_p
    \right\}.
  \]
  Choose a prime \(r\notin S\cup\{\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_K\}\) dividing
  none of the finitely many nonzero differences \(m-n\), \(m\in F\).
  Then \(q=q_0r\) is coprime to \(a_0\), and the \(q\)-neighborhood of
  \(n\) meets \(X_{K,E}\) only at \(n\). Thus precisely the points with
  fewer than \(K\) outside primes survive one derivative. Iteration
  proves the formula. Finally \(X_{0,E}\) is a nonempty finite set (it
  contains \(1\)), so the height is exactly \(K+1\).
\end{proof}

Broughan's related full-adic result counts prime factors with
multiplicity and identifies closures of the corresponding strata
\cite[Theorem~4.3]{Broughan2003}. The preceding proposition is a local
refinement of that analysis, not a new classification of the ambient adic
space: it uses distinct prime support, deletes the finite set \(S\), imposes
bounded \(S\)-adic valuations, and computes derivatives intrinsically in
the indicated subspace rather than only in its ambient closure.

\begin{theorem}[Nonunit escape dichotomy and MCF-immunity]
  \label{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}
  Let \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) be an infinite MCFL. Then either
  \[
    \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S^c}(\val_U(w))=\infty,
  \]
  or there is a prime \(p\mid a_0\) such that
  \[
    \sup_{w\in L}v_p(\val_U(w))=\infty.
  \]
  Consequently the following languages are MCF-immune whenever they are
  infinite:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item the prime representation language \(\mathcal P_U\);
    \item every language on which \(\Omega(\val_U(w))\) is bounded;
    \item every finite-prime-support language on which all
    \(v_p(\val_U(w))\), \(p\mid a_0\), are bounded.
  \end{enumerate}
  In a unit system, every bounded-\(\omega(\val_U(w))\) language is
  MCF-immune.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  If neither alternative held, finiteness of \(S\) would give
  \(K\ge0\) and \(E\in\NN_0^S\) such that
  \[
    \{\val_U(w):w\in L\}\subseteq X_{K,E}.
  \]
  By \Cref{prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank}, \(X_{K,E}\) is scattered.
  This contradicts
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint}. Prime values satisfy
  \(\omega_{S^c}\le1\) and \(v_p\le1\); bounded \(\Omega\) bounds both
  alternatives; and finite prime support bounds \(\omega_{S^c}\), with
  the remaining valuations bounded by hypothesis. If \(S=\varnothing\),
  the second alternative is absent, giving the unit-system assertion.
\end{proof}

The second alternative is necessary. In ordinary base \(b\), where
\(a_0=b\), the regular LSD-first language
\(\{0^n1:n\ge0\}\) represents \(b^n\). Its distinct prime support is
fixed, while \(v_p(b^n)\) is unbounded for every \(p\mid b\).

\begin{proposition}[Recurrence-witness inflation and strict separation]
  \label{prop:recurrence-witness-separation}
  If a tail has minimal recurrence polynomial \(\mu_U\), then for every
  prime \(p\) it also has a recurrence witness with characteristic
  polynomial \((X-p)\mu_U(X)\).  When \(\abs{\mu_U(0)}=1\), the trailing
  coefficient of this witness has absolute value \(p\).

  Moreover, there are two canonical eventually recurrent
  numeration systems with regular representation languages and recurrence
  witnesses whose trailing-coefficient prime set is \(\{p\}\), but with
  opposite bounded-outside-prime escape behavior:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item ordinary base \(p\) contains the infinite regular language
    \(\{0^n1:n\ge0\}\), whose values are \(p^n\);
    \item the Zeckendorf system, with \(F_0=0,F_1=1\) and
    \(F_{n+2}=F_{n+1}+F_n\), equipped with the order-three recurrence
    witness
    \[
      F_{n+3}=-pF_n+(1-p)F_{n+1}+(p+1)F_{n+2},
    \]
    contains no infinite MCFL \(L\) for which
    \[
      \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{\{p\}^c}(\val(w))<\infty.
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
  Thus the prime divisors of an arbitrarily selected trailing coefficient
  do not determine the escape behavior of the numeration system.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
  The polynomial identity \(\mu_U(C)z_J=0\) remains valid after
  multiplication by \(X-p\).  In the companion convention, the trailing
  recurrence coefficient is the negative of the constant term of the
  characteristic polynomial, proving the first assertion.

 succeeded in 276ms:
  \textup{(U1)--(U3)} must also be preserved. Thus general decidability,
  undecidability, and any hardness classification remain open; the proved
  effective statement is exactly the positive semidecision above. The
  Perron-recurrence subclass of greedy linear numeration bases is resolved
  separately below.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Arbitrarily deep quotient congruences]
  \label{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain}
  Suppose \(\abs{a_0}=1\), and let \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) be an
  infinite MCFL. For arbitrary sequences \(M_i\ge1\) and \(r_i\ge1\),
  \(L\) contains a strictly increasing sequence \(N_i\) such that
  \[
    N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i,\qquad Q_i>1,\qquad
    Q_i\equiv1\pmod {M_iN_i^{r_i}}.
  \]
  If \(M_i\) is divisible by every prime at most \(B_i\), then every
  prime divisor of \(Q_i\) exceeds \(B_i\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Take the fixed orbit \(N(t)\) from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, discarding finitely many
  initial parameters so that \(N(t)\ge2\). Having selected
  \(N_i=N(t_i)\), apply the synchronized congruence with
  \[
    q_i=M_iN_i^{r_i+1}.
  \]
  Set \(t_{i+1}=t_i+H(q_i)\). Strict increase and the congruence give
  \[
    N_{i+1}=N_i+c_iM_iN_i^{r_i+1}
           =N_i(1+c_iM_iN_i^{r_i})
  \]
  for an integer \(c_i\ge1\). This proves the first assertion. Under
  the last hypothesis, \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod p\) for every prime
  \(p\le B_i\), so none of those primes divides \(Q_i\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Induced full divisibility tree]
  \label{thm:unit-mcfl-divisibility-tree}
  Under the hypotheses of
  \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain}, there is an injection
  \[
    \Phi:\NN^{<\omega}\longrightarrow L
  \]
  such that
  \[
    \val_U(\Phi(\sigma))\mid\val_U(\Phi(\tau))
      \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \sigma\preceq\tau,
  \]
  where \(\preceq\) denotes the prefix order. The quotients attached to
  distinct edges can be chosen pairwise coprime. Moreover, given an
  arbitrary prescribed threshold for each edge, every prime divisor of
  its quotient can be required to exceed that threshold.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Use the fixed strictly increasing orbit from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}. Here is an explicit
  parent-before-child enumeration. If \(p_i\) is the \(i\)-th prime, encode
  \(\sigma=(\sigma_0,\ldots,\sigma_{m-1})\) by
  \[
    \kappa(\sigma)=2^m\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}p_{i+2}^{\sigma_i+1}.
  \]
  Unique factorization makes \(\kappa\) injective, and
  \(\kappa(\sigma^\frown j)>\kappa(\sigma)\). Enumerating the nonroot
  nodes, equivalently their incoming edges, by increasing \(\kappa\)
  therefore has order type \(\omega\) and places every parent before each
  child. Map
  the root to any orbit word of value \(R_0\ge2\). Suppose finitely many
  edges have been assigned, and consider a new edge from a parent of
  value \(P\). Let \(R\) be divisible by \(R_0\), by every previously
  assigned edge quotient, and by every prime not exceeding the prescribed
  threshold for the new edge. Apply the synchronized orbit congruence
  modulo \(P^2R\) at the parameter of the parent. A sufficiently large
  positive multiple of the return time gives an unused orbit word of
  value \(P'>P\) with
  \[
    P'\equiv P\pmod {P^2R}.
  \]
  Hence \(P'=PQ\), where \(Q>1\) and \(Q\equiv1\pmod {PR}\). In
  particular, \(Q\) is coprime to the root and to every earlier edge
  quotient, and no prime up to the prescribed threshold divides \(Q\).

  Recursion defines \(\Phi\) on every node. The value at a node is the
  root value times the product of the edge quotients along its path.
  Since the root and all distinct edge quotients are pairwise coprime,
  the value at \(\sigma\) divides the value at \(\tau\) exactly when the
  root-to-\(\sigma\) edge set is contained in the root-to-\(\tau\) edge
  set. For two nodes of a rooted tree, containment of their root-path edge
  sets holds exactly when the first node is an ancestor of the second;
  here this is precisely \(\sigma\preceq\tau\). This also proves injectivity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Prime immunity for recurrent numeration systems]
  \label{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity}
  Let \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\) satisfy \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, and put
  \[
    \mathcal P_U
      :=\{w\in\mathcal R_U:\val_U(w)\text{ is prime}\}.
  \]
  Then \(\mathcal P_U\) is MCF-immune, and therefore CF-immune,
  REG-immune, and not recognizable by a finite automaton over \(A\).

  If, in addition, \(\abs{a_0}=1\), then every infinite multiple
  context-free
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) has unbounded
  \(\omega(\val_U(w))\). More precisely, \(L\) contains a
  coprime-quotient chain \(N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i\) with
  \(Q_i>1\) and \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i}\). Thus the bounded-\(\omega\),
  bounded-\(\Omega\), and finite-prime-support languages of a unit
  system are MCF-immune whenever they are infinite. Without the unit
  hypothesis, every bounded-\(\Omega\) language is still MCF-immune.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  The MCF-immunity assertions follow from
  \Cref{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}, including its
  bounded-\(\Omega\) conclusion. In a unit system \(S\) is empty, so
  that theorem gives unbounded \(\omega\). Taking
  \(M_i=r_i=1\) in
  \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain} gives the stated chain.
  Regular languages are context-free and context-free languages are
  MCFLs. Finally, \textup{(U2)} and Euclid's theorem make
  \(\mathcal P_U\) infinite.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Linear numeration bases with Perron recurrence]
  \label{cor:linear-perron-prime-cf-immunity}
  Let \(U=(U_n)_{n\ge0}\) be a standard greedy linear numeration basis in
  the following precise sense: \(U_0=1\), the \(U_n\) are strictly
  increasing integers with bounded quotients, \(\mathcal R_U\) is the LSD-first
  reversal of the usual greedy representation language, and \(U\)
  has minimal tail recurrence polynomial equal to the minimal polynomial
  of a Perron number \(\beta\). Thus
  \(\beta>1\) is a real algebraic integer and every other conjugate
  \(\beta'\) satisfies \(\abs{\beta'}<\beta\); compare
  \cite{LindMarcus1995,Rigo2014Vol2}. Then its prime
  representation language is MCF-immune, hence CF-immune, REG-immune and
  nonrecognizable. The same statements hold in the usual MSD-first
  convention.

  If the dominant Perron number is a unit and the chosen recurrence has
  trailing coefficient \(a_0=\pm1\), then the full coprime-quotient and
  bounded-prime-support MCF-immunity conclusions of
  \Cref{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity} hold.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  Greedy expansion gives a unique representation of every positive
  integer, and its length-\(m\) words represent values between successive
  place-value thresholds; thus \textup{(U1)--(U3)} hold. The defining
  Perron recurrence gives \textup{(U4)}. Apply
  \Cref{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity}. The immunity proof
  uses only \textup{(U1)--(U4)} and does not assume that the full greedy
  language is regular. In the Pisot subclass, Frougny's finite
  normalization theorem supplies a finite transducer, and the associated
  canonical syntax and Bertrand/substitution descriptions are standard
  \cite{Frougny1992,BruyereHansel1997,Fabre1995,FrougnySolomyak1992}.
  Finally, multiple context-free, context-free, and
  regular languages are closed under reversal, so changing between the LSD-first proof
  convention and the usual MSD-first convention preserves every
  conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[Pisot and non-Pisot Perron systems]
  \label{ex:pisot-unit-nonunit}
  The Fibonacci recurrence has characteristic polynomial
  \(X^2-X-1\), the Pell recurrence has \(X^2-2X-1\), and the
  Tribonacci recurrence has \(X^3-X^2-X-1\). Their trailing recurrence
  coefficient is \(a_0=1\), so the iterated conclusion applies. In
  ordinary base \(b\), \(U_n=b^n\) and \(a_0=b\); prime MCF-immunity
  follows, strengthening the classical fixed-base CF-immunity conclusion
  to MCF-immunity, but the unit iteration is not being asserted.

  A genuinely nonintegral, nonunit Pisot example is obtained from
  \[
    U_0=1,\qquad U_1=3,\qquad
    U_{n+2}=2U_{n+1}+2U_n.
  \]
  If \(2<U_{n+1}/U_n\le3\), then
  \[
    \frac{U_{n+2}}{U_{n+1}}
      =2+\frac{2U_n}{U_{n+1}}\in[8/3,3),
  \]
  so induction from \(U_1/U_0=3\) proves strict increase and bounded
  quotients. The irreducible polynomial \(X^2-2X-2\) annihilates this
  nonzero rational sequence, hence is its minimal tail polynomial. Its
  dominant root is \(1+\sqrt3\), and its trailing coefficient is
  \(a_0=2\), so the action is singular modulo \(2\). Nevertheless,
  \Cref{cor:linear-perron-bounded-support-classification} shows that every
  infinite MCFL in this nonintegral system acquires unbounded prime support
  away from \(2\); singularity of the ambient action does not produce a
  valuation-only escape language.

  The polynomial \(X^2-5X+5\) gives a genuinely non-Pisot instance. Its
  dominant root \(\beta=(5+\sqrt5)/2\) is Perron, while its other conjugate
  \((5-\sqrt5)/2\) is greater than one. The integer sequence
  \[
    U_0=1,\qquad U_1=4,\qquad
    U_{n+2}=5U_{n+1}-5U_n
  \]
  begins \(1,4,15,55,200,\ldots\) and is strictly increasing. Indeed,
  \[
    U_{n+1}>3U_n>0
  \]
  holds at \(n=0\), and if it holds at \(n\), then
  \[
    U_{n+2}=5(U_{n+1}-U_n)
      >\frac{10}{3}U_{n+1}>3U_{n+1}.
  \]
  Moreover, if \(3<U_{n+1}/U_n\le4\), then
  \[
    \frac{U_{n+2}}{U_{n+1}}
      =5-\frac{5U_n}{U_{n+1}}
      \le 5-\frac54<4,
  \]
  while the lower bound was just proved; starting from \(U_1/U_0=4\)
  therefore gives \(3<U_{n+1}/U_n\le4\) for every \(n\). Thus the quotients
  are bounded, so its greedy language is the greedy language of a linear
  numeration basis with Perron recurrence in the stated sense. The
  classification below implies that every infinite MCFL in this standard
  greedy language has unbounded prime support away from \(5\).
\end{example}

\begin{corollary}[Bounded support for Perron-recurrence numeration]
  \label{cor:linear-perron-bounded-support-classification}
  Let \(U\) be a greedy linear numeration basis in the precise sense of
  \Cref{cor:linear-perron-prime-cf-immunity}, let \(\beta\) be its dominant
  Perron number, and let \(S_{\mathrm r}\) be the prime divisors of the
  constant coefficient of its minimal tail recurrence.  The following are
  equivalent:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item there is an infinite MCFL \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) such that
    \[
      \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(\val_U(w))<\infty;
    \]
    \item \(U\) admits a geometric synchronized scheme;
    \item \(\beta\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\).
  \end{enumerate}
  Consequently, if \(\beta\) is nonintegral, every infinite MCFL
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) has unbounded prime support outside
  \(S_{\mathrm r}\).  For effective input as in
  \Cref{cor:bounded-outside-support-semidecidable}, the three equivalent
  properties are decidable in this Perron-recurrence class: the answer is positive
  exactly when the supplied minimal polynomial has degree one.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  The equivalence of \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} is
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}.  Suppose that
  \textup{(ii)} holds, write
  \[
    \val_U(W(t))=cb^t,
    \qquad \abs{W(t)}=L+Dt,
  \]
  where \(D>0\) is the total pumped length.  In a greedy \(U\)-system, a
  canonical word of length \(m\) represents a value in the half-open interval
  \([U_{m-1},U_m)\).  Hence
  \[
    U_{L+Dt-1}\le cb^t<U_{L+Dt}.
  \]
  The standard solution of the recurrence defined by the minimal polynomial
  of the Perron number gives
  \[
    \lim_{n\to\infty}U_n^{1/n}=\beta.
  \]
  Here is the required nonvanishing argument. Over the splitting field the
  minimal polynomial is separable and the eventual expansion has the form
  \(U_n=\sum_\gamma A_\gamma\gamma^n\), with \(\gamma\) running over the
  conjugates of \(\beta\). Since \(U_n\in\mathbb Q\), Galois automorphisms
  send \(A_\beta\) to the corresponding \(A_{\sigma(\beta)}\). If
  \(A_\beta=0\), every conjugate coefficient would vanish, contradicting
  that this polynomial is the minimal tail annihilator of the nonzero
  sequence. Thus \(A_\beta\ne0\), and every other conjugate has modulus
  strictly less than \(\beta\), proving the limit. Taking
  \(t\)-th roots in the preceding inequalities therefore yields
  \(b=\beta^D\).

  If \(\beta\) had degree greater than one, any other conjugate
  \(\beta'\) would satisfy \((\beta')^D=b\), and hence
  \(\abs{\beta'}=b^{1/D}=\beta\), contrary to the defining Perron
  inequality.  Thus \(\beta\) has degree one; being an algebraic integer
  greater than one, it belongs to \(\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\).  This proves
  \textup{(ii)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(iii)}.

  Conversely, suppose \(\beta=B\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\).  Its minimal
  polynomial is \(X-B\), so, after the minimal recurrence threshold
  \(J_{\mathrm r}\), one has \(U_{n+1}=BU_n\).  The greedy representation
  of \(U_n\), in the LSD-first convention, is \(0^n1\).  Taking
  \[
    a=0^{J_{\mathrm r}},\quad k=1,\quad
    u_1=w_1=s_1=\varepsilon,\quad v_1=0,\quad u_2=1
  \]
  gives canonical words \(W(t)=0^{J_{\mathrm r}+t}1\) and
  \[
    \val_U(W(t))=U_{J_{\mathrm r}}B^t.
  \]
  This is a geometric synchronized scheme, proving
  \textup{(iii)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(ii)}.  The universal assertion
  for nonintegral \(\beta\) is the negation of \textup{(i)}.  Finally, a
  Perron minimal polynomial has degree one exactly when its root is an
  integer, which gives the stated terminating decision procedure.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Exact open interfaces]
  \label{rem:pisot-open-interfaces}
  The terminology for linear numeration systems with Perron or Pisot
  recurrence is not uniform.
  \Cref{cor:linear-perron-prime-cf-immunity} covers the standard

 succeeded in 282ms:
\begin{definition}[Geometric synchronized scheme]
  \label{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}
  A geometric synchronized scheme for \(U\) consists of
  \(a\in A^{J_{\mathrm r}}\), an integer \(k\ge1\), integers
  \(c\ge1,b\ge2\), and words
  \[
    u_1,\ldots,u_{k+1},\quad
    v_1,\ldots,v_k,\quad w_1,\ldots,w_k,\quad
    s_1,\ldots,s_k\in A^\ast,
  \]
  such that
  \[
    W(t)=a u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^tu_2\cdots
      u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}\in\mathcal R_U
      \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  \[
    \sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0,
    \qquad \val_U(W(t))=cb^t\quad(t\ge0).
  \]
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Arithmetic input of Evertse]
  \label{lem:evertse-quotient-support-input}
  Let \(K\) be a number field. For \(x\in K^\times\), let \(P_K(x)\)
  be the largest norm of a finite prime ideal \(\mathfrak p\) for which
  \(\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak p}(x)\ne0\), with \(P_K(x)=1\) when
  \(x\) is a unit. If \(Z=(Z(n))_{n\ge0}\) is a nondegenerate linear
  recurrence over \(K\) whose minimal shift-annihilating polynomial has
  at least two distinct nonzero roots, then
  \[
    \lim_{\substack{r\to\infty\\ r>s\ge0,\ Z(s)\ne0}}
      P_K\!\left(\frac{Z(r)}{Z(s)}\right)=\infty.
  \]
  In particular, a rational-integer recurrence with no zero terms, all
  supported on one finite set of rational primes, cannot be both
  nondegenerate and have at least two characteristic roots.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  The displayed uniform limit is exactly
  \cite[Theorem~3]{Evertse1984}. For the consequence, let \(S\) be the
  finite rational-prime support and choose a number field \(K\) containing
  the characteristic roots and coefficients. If \(Z(r),Z(s)\ne0\), the
  principal fractional ideal generated by \(Z(r)/Z(s)\) is supported
  only at prime ideals above primes in \(S\). Every prime ideal
  \(\mathfrak p\) above \(p\in S\) has
  \[
    \operatorname N\mathfrak p=p^{f(\mathfrak p/p)}
      \le p^{[K:\mathbb Q]}.
  \]
  Consequently
  \[
    P_K\!\left(\frac{Z(r)}{Z(s)}\right)
      \le \max\bigl(\{p^{[K:\mathbb Q]}:p\in S\}\cup\{1\}\bigr)
  \]
  for every \(r,s\) with nonzero denominator. This fixed bound
  contradicts Evertse's limit when there are at least two characteristic
  roots. Here and below, ``characteristic roots of a sequence'' always
  means the roots of its minimal shift-annihilating polynomial, not roots
  introduced by a nonminimal recurrence witness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Increasing \(S\)-unit recurrences]
  \label{lem:s-unit-increasing-recurrence-rigidity}
  Let \(Y=(Y(n))_{n\ge0}\) be a strictly increasing sequence of positive
  rational integers satisfying a linear recurrence. If all prime
  divisors of all \(Y(n)\) belong to one finite set, then there are
  integers \(r\ge0,h\ge1,c\ge1\), and \(b\ge2\) such that
  \[
    Y(r+hn)=cb^n\qquad(n\ge0).
  \]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  Write the standard exponential-polynomial expansion, after discarding
  the contribution of the zero characteristic root and a finite initial
  segment, as
  \[
    Y(n)=\sum_{i=1}^m f_i(n)\alpha_i^n,
  \]
  where the \(\alpha_i\) are distinct nonzero algebraic numbers and the
  nonzero \(f_i\) are algebraic polynomials. Partition the roots by
  \(\alpha_i\sim\alpha_j\) when \(\alpha_i/\alpha_j\) is a root of
  unity. Choose \(h_0\ge1\) divisible by the orders of all such
  quotients. For each \(j\in\{0,\ldots,h_0-1\}\), substitution of
  \(n=j+h_0t\) and collection within the equivalence classes gives
  \[
    Y(j+h_0t)=\sum_C g_{C,j}(t)\beta_C^t,
    \qquad \beta_C=\alpha_i^{h_0}\quad(i\in C).
  \]
  Delete the identically zero summands. Distinct remaining \(\beta_C\)
  have quotient that is not a root of unity. Thus every residue
  subsequence is a nonzero nondegenerate recurrence; it is nonzero
  because all of its terms are positive.

  Every term of every residue subsequence is a nonzero rational integer
  supported on the prescribed finite set. If a residue subsequence had
  at least two characteristic roots, the consequence in
  \Cref{lem:evertse-quotient-support-input} would contradict the fixed
  support of all its term quotients. Hence every residue subsequence has
  exactly one characteristic root and therefore has the form
  \[
    Z(n)=f(n)\lambda^n
  \]
  with one characteristic root.

  Choose one residue subsequence and shift its index if necessary so
  that the displayed expansion holds from \(n=0\), and put
  \(e=\deg f\). Let \(E\) denote the forward shift. Over
  \(\overline{\mathbb Q}\), finite differences give
  \[
    (E-\lambda)^{e+1}Z=0,
    \qquad (E-\lambda)^eZ\ne0,
  \]
  because the \(e\)-th difference of the nonzero degree-\(e\) polynomial
  \(f\) is a nonzero constant. Hence the monic minimal shift-annihilator
  over \(\overline{\mathbb Q}\) is exactly
  \((X-\lambda)^{e+1}\), and the Hankel rank of \(Z\) over that field is
  \(e+1\).

  The Hankel matrix has rational entries, and a rational matrix has the
  same rank over \(\mathbb Q\) and over \(\overline{\mathbb Q}\).
  Therefore a minimal dependence among its shift columns gives a monic
  polynomial \(P\in\mathbb Q[X]\) of the same degree \(e+1\). After
  extending scalars, minimality and equality of degrees force
  \[
    P(X)=(X-\lambda)^{e+1}.
  \]
  For every
  \(\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}/\mathbb Q)\), the
  polynomial \(P\) is fixed and has only the root \(\lambda\); thus
  \(\sigma(\lambda)=\lambda\). Hence \(\lambda\in\mathbb Q\). Now
  \(f(n)=Z(n)/\lambda^n\in\mathbb Q\)
  for every \(n\), and interpolation from \(\deg(f)+1\) integer
  arguments gives \(f\in\mathbb Q[X]\). If \(f\) were nonconstant, clear its
  denominators to obtain a nonconstant \(g\in\mathbb Z[X]\). The
  identity \(Z(n)=f(n)\lambda^n\) would confine every prime divisor of
  every nonzero \(g(n)\) to a fixed finite set. Indeed, if
  \(\lambda=a/b\) is in lowest terms and \(g=Df\), then
  \[
    g(n)a^n=Db^nZ(n).
  \]
  Thus that fixed set is the union of the prescribed prime set and the
  prime divisors of \(Dab\). This contradicts Schur's theorem that a
  nonconstant integer polynomial has values divisible by infinitely
  many primes; see, for example, \cite{HardyWright2008}. Hence \(f\) is
  constant.

  We have therefore \(Z(n)=c\lambda^n\), where
  \(c=Z(0)\) is a positive integer. If \(\lambda=a/b\) in lowest terms,
  integrality for every \(n\) forces \(b^n\mid c\) for every \(n\), so
  \(b=1\). Positivity and strict increase then force
  \(\lambda\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\). Translating the chosen residue and
  index shift back to \(Y\) supplies the asserted \(r\) and \(h\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Geometric-ray characterization]
  \label{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}
  For a canonical eventually recurrent numeration system satisfying
  \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, the following are equivalent.
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item There are a finite \(k\) and an infinite \(k\)-MCFL
    \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) such that
    \[
      \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(\val_U(w))<\infty.
    \]
    \item The system admits a geometric synchronized scheme in the
    sense of \Cref{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}.
    \item The language \(\mathcal R_U\) contains an infinite linear MCFL
    \(L_0=\{W(t):t\ge0\}\) of finite fan-out for which there are
    \(c\ge1,b\ge2\) satisfying
    \[
      \val_U(W(t))=cb^t\qquad(t\ge0).
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose first that \textup{(i)} holds. Use the minimal tail recurrence
  as the witness in
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, and write its resulting
  ray and strictly increasing value sequence as \(W(t)\) and \(N(t)\).
  Let
  \[
    K_0=\max_{t\ge0}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(N(t)),
  \]
  and choose \(t_0\) attaining the maximum. Let \(T\) be the set of
  primes outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\) that divide \(N(t_0)\), and put
  \(q=\prod_{\ell\in T}\ell\). If \(T\ne\varnothing\), take the return
  time \(H=H(q)\) from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}; if \(T=\varnothing\),
  put \(H=1\). Every prime in \(T\) divides every \(N(t_0+nH)\). A prime
  divisor outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\cup T\) would therefore give at least
  \(K_0+1\) distinct prime divisors outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\), contrary
  to the definition of \(K_0\). Thus all terms of
  \[
    Y(n)=N(t_0+nH)
  \]
  are supported on the fixed finite set \(S_{\mathrm r}\cup T\).

  To make the termwise-product closure explicit, put
  \(n_0=d_{\mathrm r}+1\), list the \(2k\) powered affine blocks as
  \(B_1,\ldots,B_{2k}\), and form
  \[
    K=B_1\otimes\cdots\otimes B_{2k}.
  \]
  Since \(K^t=B_1^t\otimes\cdots\otimes B_{2k}^t\), expansion of the
  interspersed constant block matrices expresses \(N(t)\) as a fixed
  linear functional of \(K^t\). Cayley--Hamilton therefore gives a
  recurrence of order at most
  \[
    R=n_0^{2k}=(d_{\mathrm r}+1)^{2k}.
  \]
  Arithmetic subsequences of a recurrence are recurrences, so \(Y\) is a
  strictly increasing positive integer recurrence. By
  \Cref{lem:s-unit-increasing-recurrence-rigidity}, there are
  \(r\ge0,h\ge1,c\ge1,b\ge2\) such that
  \(N(t_\ast+h_\ast n)=cb^n\), where
  \(t_\ast=t_0+Hr\) and \(h_\ast=Hh\).

  In the synchronized factorization, replace
  \[
    u_j\ \text{by}\ u_jv_j^{t_\ast},\qquad
    v_j\ \text{by}\ v_j^{h_\ast},\qquad
    w_j\ \text{by}\ w_js_j^{t_\ast},\qquad
    s_j\ \text{by}\ s_j^{h_\ast}.
  \]
  This absorbs the fixed powers and parametrizes exactly the subray
  \(W(t_\ast+h_\ast n)\). Its total pumped length remains positive, so
  it is a geometric synchronized scheme. This proves
  \textup{(i)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(ii)}.

  The implication \textup{(ii)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(iii)} is
  \Cref{lem:synchronized-ray-linear-mcfg}. Finally, under
  \textup{(iii)}, the language \(L_0\) is an infinite MCFL and
  \[
    \omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(cb^t)\le\omega(cb)
    \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  proving \textup{(i)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Prime support of the geometric ratio]
  \label{lem:geometric-ratio-tail-support}
  In every geometric synchronized scheme
  \(\val_U(W(t))=cb^t\), each prime divisor of \(b\) divides the
  constant coefficient \(c_0\) of the minimal tail recurrence.  Equivalently,
  \[
    \operatorname{rad}(b)\mid\operatorname{rad}(\abs{c_0}).
  \]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose that a prime \(p\mid b\) does not divide \(c_0\), put
  \(e=v_p(c)\), and take the allowed modulus \(q=p^{e+1}\).  Every
  nonempty block matrix among the \(M_{v_j}\) and \(M_{s_j}\) is invertible
  modulo \(q\) by \Cref{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}.  A common
  multiple \(H\ge1\) of their orders gives, by the matrix product defining
  the scheme exactly as in the proof of
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit},
  \[
    \val_U(W(t+H))\equiv\val_U(W(t))\pmod q
    \qquad(t\ge0).
  \]
  At \(t=0\) this says
  \[
    cb^H\equiv c\pmod {p^{e+1}}.
  \]
  But \(p\mid b\) implies \(p\nmid b^H-1\), so
  \(v_p(c(b^H-1))=e\), contradicting the displayed congruence.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Positive semidecidability on promised presentations]
  \label{cor:bounded-outside-support-semidecidable}
  On the promised class of valid presentations satisfying
  \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, suppose the finite digit alphabet, recurrence and
  initial data, and a DFA recognizing \(\mathcal R_U\) are supplied,
  together with a supplied or effectively certified minimal tail
  recurrence and a valid threshold \(J_{\mathrm r}\). Then the set of
  positive promised instances of the property in
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}\textup{(i)} is
  recursively enumerable, equivalently it has a \(\Sigma^0_1\) upper
  bound. No recognition procedure for membership in the promised input
  class is asserted.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  Enumerate \(k\ge1\), \(a\in A^{J_{\mathrm r}}\), and all finite tuples
  of words occurring in
  \Cref{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}, rejecting tuples of zero
  total pumped length. For a fixed tuple, whether the DFA accepts
  \(W(t)\) for every \(t\ge0\) is decidable: the tuple of powers of the
  finitely many pumped-block transformations is eventually periodic in
  the finite transition monoid, and its preperiod and period are found
  by exhaustive iteration.

  The affine matrices construct an exact linear recurrence for
  \[
    N(t)=\val_U(W(t)).
  \]
  With \(d_{\mathrm r}\) the minimal tail order, the Kronecker construction
  in the proof of
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray} has dimension
  \[
    R=(d_{\mathrm r}+1)^{2k};
  \]
  it is computable from the candidate and is an upper bound for the
  recurrence order of \(N(t)\). For a tuple accepted at every parameter,
  compute \(c=N(0)\). The only
  possible integer ratio is \(b=N(1)/N(0)\). Check that
  \(b\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\), and then decide whether the recurrence
  \(N(t+1)-bN(t)\) is identically zero by checking its first \(R\) terms;
  Cayley--Hamilton makes those \(R\) zero values sufficient. Thus each
  candidate scheme is decidable, and the enumeration halts exactly on
  positive promised instances by
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}. No claim of a
  decision procedure for negative instances is made.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Exact algorithmic boundary]
  \label{rem:general-decision-interface}
  The preceding corollary does not yield a decision procedure. To turn
  its enumeration into one, it would suffice to compute from the input
  DFA and minimal tail recurrence a bound \(B\) such that every positive
  instance has a geometric synchronized scheme with fan-out and total
  constant and pumped-block length at most \(B\). No such compression
  follows from DFA pumping: replacing a long loop by a shorter word with
  the same DFA endpoints need not preserve its affine recurrence matrix,
  and can therefore destroy the identity

 succeeded in 190ms:
  \(c=Z(0)\) is a positive integer. If \(\lambda=a/b\) in lowest terms,
  integrality for every \(n\) forces \(b^n\mid c\) for every \(n\), so
  \(b=1\). Positivity and strict increase then force
  \(\lambda\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\). Translating the chosen residue and
  index shift back to \(Y\) supplies the asserted \(r\) and \(h\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Geometric-ray characterization]
  \label{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}
  For a canonical eventually recurrent numeration system satisfying
  \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, the following are equivalent.
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item There are a finite \(k\) and an infinite \(k\)-MCFL
    \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) such that
    \[
      \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(\val_U(w))<\infty.
    \]
    \item The system admits a geometric synchronized scheme in the
    sense of \Cref{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}.
    \item The language \(\mathcal R_U\) contains an infinite linear MCFL
    \(L_0=\{W(t):t\ge0\}\) of finite fan-out for which there are
    \(c\ge1,b\ge2\) satisfying
    \[
      \val_U(W(t))=cb^t\qquad(t\ge0).
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose first that \textup{(i)} holds. Use the minimal tail recurrence
  as the witness in
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, and write its resulting
  ray and strictly increasing value sequence as \(W(t)\) and \(N(t)\).
  Let
  \[
    K_0=\max_{t\ge0}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(N(t)),
  \]
  and choose \(t_0\) attaining the maximum. Let \(T\) be the set of
  primes outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\) that divide \(N(t_0)\), and put
  \(q=\prod_{\ell\in T}\ell\). If \(T\ne\varnothing\), take the return
  time \(H=H(q)\) from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}; if \(T=\varnothing\),
  put \(H=1\). Every prime in \(T\) divides every \(N(t_0+nH)\). A prime
  divisor outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\cup T\) would therefore give at least
  \(K_0+1\) distinct prime divisors outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\), contrary
  to the definition of \(K_0\). Thus all terms of
  \[
    Y(n)=N(t_0+nH)
  \]
  are supported on the fixed finite set \(S_{\mathrm r}\cup T\).

  To make the termwise-product closure explicit, put
  \(n_0=d_{\mathrm r}+1\), list the \(2k\) powered affine blocks as
  \(B_1,\ldots,B_{2k}\), and form
  \[
    K=B_1\otimes\cdots\otimes B_{2k}.
  \]
  Since \(K^t=B_1^t\otimes\cdots\otimes B_{2k}^t\), expansion of the
  interspersed constant block matrices expresses \(N(t)\) as a fixed
  linear functional of \(K^t\). Cayley--Hamilton therefore gives a
  recurrence of order at most
  \[
    R=n_0^{2k}=(d_{\mathrm r}+1)^{2k}.
  \]
  Arithmetic subsequences of a recurrence are recurrences, so \(Y\) is a
  strictly increasing positive integer recurrence. By
  \Cref{lem:s-unit-increasing-recurrence-rigidity}, there are
  \(r\ge0,h\ge1,c\ge1,b\ge2\) such that
  \(N(t_\ast+h_\ast n)=cb^n\), where
  \(t_\ast=t_0+Hr\) and \(h_\ast=Hh\).

  In the synchronized factorization, replace
  \[
    u_j\ \text{by}\ u_jv_j^{t_\ast},\qquad
    v_j\ \text{by}\ v_j^{h_\ast},\qquad
    w_j\ \text{by}\ w_js_j^{t_\ast},\qquad
    s_j\ \text{by}\ s_j^{h_\ast}.
  \]
  This absorbs the fixed powers and parametrizes exactly the subray
  \(W(t_\ast+h_\ast n)\). Its total pumped length remains positive, so
  it is a geometric synchronized scheme. This proves
  \textup{(i)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(ii)}.

  The implication \textup{(ii)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(iii)} is
  \Cref{lem:synchronized-ray-linear-mcfg}. Finally, under
  \textup{(iii)}, the language \(L_0\) is an infinite MCFL and
  \[
    \omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(cb^t)\le\omega(cb)
    \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  proving \textup{(i)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Prime support of the geometric ratio]
  \label{lem:geometric-ratio-tail-support}
  In every geometric synchronized scheme
  \(\val_U(W(t))=cb^t\), each prime divisor of \(b\) divides the
  constant coefficient \(c_0\) of the minimal tail recurrence.  Equivalently,
  \[
    \operatorname{rad}(b)\mid\operatorname{rad}(\abs{c_0}).

# Literature and Novelty Check

Checked through 2026-08-15.  The arXiv searches below used the public Atom API

The arXiv API search is negative evidence only.  In particular, the older
JACM papers settle a stronger fixed-base question than their titles alone
suggest, so the paper does not advertise fixed-base CF-immunity as new.

## Exact classical citations

- William Ogden, "A helpful result for proving inherent ambiguity,"
  *Mathematical Systems Theory* 2 (1968), 191--194,
  DOI 10.1007/BF01694004.  This is the marked-position pumping lemma now
  called Ogden's lemma.
- William Ogden, Rockford J. Ross, and Karl Winklmann, "An 'Interchange
  Lemma' for Context-Free Languages," *SIAM Journal on Computing* 14 (1985),
  410--415, DOI 10.1137/0214031.  The present proof does not claim or reproduce
  their interchange lemma; ordinary context-free pumping suffices after a
  fixed-prefix quotient, while Ogden's lemma gives an alternative way to
  localize pumping beyond an eventual-recurrence transient.
- J. Hartmanis and H. Shank, "On the Recognition of Primes by Automata,"
  *Journal of the ACM* 15 (1968), 382--389,
  DOI 10.1145/321466.321470.
- M. P. Schutzenberger, "A Remark on Acceptable Sets of Numbers,"
  *Journal of the ACM* 15 (1968), 300--303,
  DOI 10.1145/321450.321461.  Contemporary citation records describe the
  Hartmanis--Shank and Schutzenberger conclusion as: no infinite set of
  base-`b` primes is recognized by a finite automaton or a pushdown automaton.
  Thus fixed-base prime CF-immunity is prior art.
- M. Minsky and S. Papert, "Unrecognizable Sets of Numbers,"
  *Journal of the ACM* 13 (1966), 281--286,
  DOI 10.1145/321328.321337.
- D. P. Allen, Jr., "On a characterization of the nonregular set of primes,"
  *Journal of Computer and System Sciences* 2 (1968), 464--467,
  DOI 10.1016/S0022-0000(68)80038-8.

## Pisot and numeration references

- Christiane Frougny, "Representations of numbers and finite automata,"
  *Mathematical Systems Theory* 25 (1992), 37--60,
  DOI 10.1007/BF01368783.  This is the normalization/finite-transducer source
  used for linear numeration systems whose characteristic polynomial has the
  Pisot property.
- Veronique Bruyere and Georges Hansel, "Bertrand numeration systems and
  recognizability," *Theoretical Computer Science* 181 (1997), 17--43,
  DOI 10.1016/S0304-3975(96)00260-5.
- Christiane Frougny and Boris Solomyak, "Finite beta-expansions,"
  *Ergodic Theory and Dynamical Systems* 12 (1992), 713--723,
  DOI 10.1017/S0143385700007057.
- Stephane Fabre, "Substitutions et beta-systemes de numeration,"
  *Theoretical Computer Science* 137 (1995), 219--236,
  DOI 10.1016/0304-3975(95)91132-A.
- P. B. A. Lecomte and M. Rigo, "Numeration Systems on a Regular Language,"
  *Theory of Computing Systems* 34 (2001), 27--44,
  DOI 10.1007/S002240010014.  This is cited to distinguish abstract
  numeration systems from the narrower linear Pisot `U`-systems proved here.

## Novelty boundary used in the manuscript

The following assertions are treated as new only relative to the literature
located above and are stated with their full hypotheses:

1. The two-pump block-action congruence modulo the square of the represented
   value and the resulting coprime-quotient chain for an arbitrary
   context-free sublanguage of canonical Zeckendorf representations.
2. CF-immunity of the Zeckendorf prime language, and the stronger unbounded
   distinct-prime-divisor consequences for context-free Zeckendorf
   sublanguages.
3. Prime REG- and CF-immunity for the explicitly defined class of positional
   linear Pisot `U`-systems: integer weights, finite digit alphabet, injective
   canonical representations ordered by length, and an eventual integral
   recurrence with nonzero trailing coefficient.  The proof uses only the
   recurrence block action; Frougny normalization supplies the standard
   finite-state canonical syntax/normalization interface for Pisot
   `U`-systems.

No claim is made for every object sometimes called a "Pisot numeration
system."  In particular, arbitrary real-base beta-shifts, non-positional
abstract numeration systems, systems without an eventual integral recurrence,
and non-injective redundant representations are explicit open interfaces.
The Pisot-unit hypothesis is needed for the unrestricted deep-quotient and
induced-tree conclusions and for unconditional bounded-omega immunity. For a
nonunit recurrence, primes dividing the trailing coefficient make the
companion action singular modulo some composite values. The local-adic
dichotomy nevertheless gives prime and bounded-Omega MCF-immunity; bounded
distinct-prime support can escape only through unbounded valuation at a prime
dividing the trailing coefficient.

## Multiple-context-free and adic extension (2026-08-03)

The public arXiv Atom endpoint at https://export.arxiv.org/api/query was queried
with the following literal searches (and with a single-record
search_query=id:2509.02117 control):

- all:"multiple context-free" AND all:pumping;
- all:"multiple context-free" AND all:prime;
- all:"adic topology" AND all:integers;
- all:"Cantor-Bendixson" AND all:"prime factors";
- all:"divisibility tree" AND all:language.

Every API request returned HTTP 429 after retries with an identifying user
agent and four-second spacing. These failures are not treated as null search
results. The corresponding arXiv web searches, Crossref bibliographic
searches, DBLP metadata, and exact-source checks located no paper asserting an
MCFL prime-immunity theorem for recurrence numeration, an MCFL-induced local
congruence orbit, or the induced divisibility-tree conclusion.

The following published inputs delimit what is not new:

- H. Seki, T. Matsumura, M. Fujii, and T. Kasami, "On multiple
  context-free grammars," *Theoretical Computer Science* 88 (1991),
  191--229, DOI 10.1016/0304-3975(91)90374-B. Its Lemma 3.2 is the weak
  pumping lemma supplying one synchronized family with 2k pumped factors.
- M. Kanazawa, G. M. Kobele, J. Michaelis, S. Salvati, and R. Yoshinaka,
  "The Failure of the Strong Pumping Lemma for Multiple Context-Free
  Languages," *Theory of Computing Systems* 55 (2014), 250--278,
  DOI 10.1007/S00224-014-9534-Z.
- A. Duncan, M. Elder, L. Frenkel, and M. Lyu, "A substitution lemma for
  multiple context-free languages," *International Journal of Algebra and
  Computation* (2026), DOI 10.1142/S0218196726500463,
  arXiv:2509.02117v4. The direct arXiv PDF was checked. Its Lemma 5.3 quotes
  the exact Seki weak-pumping factorization used in the manuscript, while
  Theorem 4.5 gives an every-sufficiently-marked-word alternative between
  bounded pumping and replacement from one of finitely many switchable-tuple
  families. The latter does not parameterize the replacements as simultaneous
  powers of fixed blocks and therefore does not itself provide the fixed
  affine matrix-power orbit or a return time uniform in the orbit parameter.
- K. A. Broughan, "Adic Topologies for the Rational Integers," *Canadian
  Journal of Mathematics* 55 (2003), 711--723,
  DOI 10.4153/CJM-2003-030-3. The Cambridge primary PDF was checked.
  Theorem 2.4 gives the rational-space classification, Corollary 3.1 gives
  the Cantor completion, and Theorem 4.3 treats full-adic prime-factor
  strata counted with multiplicity.

Accordingly, the manuscript cites the Seki pumping family and Broughan's
topological classifications as inputs. The claims retained as new are the
combination with a fixed recurrence block orbit, the deleted-prime intrinsic
Cantor--Bendixson rank for distinct prime support with bounded local
valuations, the nonunit escape dichotomy and MCF-immunity consequences, and
the unit-system arbitrary-depth quotient and induced-tree constructions.

The arithmetic comparison now also includes Y. Bugeaud and J.-H. Evertse,
"S-parts of terms of integer linear recurrence sequences," *Mathematika* 63
(2017), 840--851, DOI 10.1112/S0025579317000298. It studies the size of the
fixed-\(S\) part of recurrence terms; it does not state a synchronized-language
characterization. The manuscript therefore presents its Evertse--Schur lemma
as a classical arithmetic input organized for the language interface, not as
an independent priority claim.

For the retained supplementary finite-state results, Shen (2022) supplies a
qualitative ordinary-base finite-automaton obstruction. Dubbe (2025) instead
lets the automaton grow with the cutoff and proves that exact prime
recognition below \(x\) requires at least
\(x\exp(-c(\log\log x)^2\log\log\log x)\) states. The supplement fixes one
DFA first and gives lengthwise symmetric-difference and recall/precision
bounds, including a Zeckendorf analogue. It does not improve Dubbe's growing
state-complexity bound, and those ordinary-base results do not supply the
fixed-DFA residue-class error statement or its Zeckendorf version.

## Refreshed prior-art and decision-target audit (2026-08-08)

The two referee-critical classical records were rechecked against their
publisher metadata and indexed abstracts, rather than inferred from their
titles. Crossref gives DOI 10.1145/321466.321470 for Hartmanis--Shank and DOI
10.1145/321450.321461 for Schutzenberger. The Hartmanis--Shank abstract states
that neither the primes nor any infinite subset of the primes, in fixed-base
notation, is accepted by a pushdown or finite automaton. The Schutzenberger
abstract states that the two negative results on acceptable sets are extended
to arbitrary context-free languages. CORE's Cornell record reproduces the
Hartmanis--Shank abstract; Semantic Scholar and Unpaywall confirm the records
and publisher copies. The manuscript already cites both papers where it calls
fixed-base prime CF-immunity classical and explicitly says that their proofs
are not reproduced as new.

The reference lists supplied by Crossref were also inspected. They lead to
the already cited Minsky--Papert paper on unrecognizable sets, the classical
context-free-language sources, and the contemporary finite-automata work.
No item in those lists concerns Zeckendorf or Pisot numeration, MCFLs, a
recurrence block action, or the geometric-ray criterion.

A live arXiv API query for `all:"multiple context-free" AND all:prime`
returned zero records. Google Scholar searches for `"primes" "multiple
context-free" language`, `"Pisot numeration" prime language automata`, and
`"S-unit" "linear recurrence" geometric progression` returned the standard
MCFG pumping literature, general numeration references, and recurrence/S-unit
sources, but no prime-language result in Pisot numeration. Crossref searches
confirmed the two 1968 JACM papers and their citation trails. A zbMATH API
keyword search for `multiple context-free prime numeration` returned no
record. OpenAlex exhausted its anonymous daily query budget during the audit,
and the first Semantic Scholar search was rate-limited; these failures are
recorded as failures, not as negative results.

The proposed decision target does not define a new problem for the class
proved in the manuscript. Theorem~3.18 proves that the prime representation
language of every system satisfying (U1)--(U4) is MCF-immune, and Euclid's
theorem plus canonical representation makes that language infinite.
Consequently it is never an MCFL. For effectively presented regular canonical
linear Pisot U-systems, the requested terminating algorithm is therefore the
constant algorithm returning `NO`; this is an immediate corollary, not a
tier-raising decision theorem.

The genuinely nontrivial nearby decision problem is whether the bounded-
outside-support condition in Theorem~3.14 holds, equivalently whether a
geometric synchronized scheme exists. Corollary~3.15 gives only positive
semidecidability. A full decision procedure would require an effective finite
bound on the number and lengths of synchronized pumped blocks, or an
equivalent negative certificate for the absence of exact geometric identities
among recurrence sequences generated by finite transition and affine-matrix
semigroups. No such bound follows from the cited pumping or recurrence
theorems; general zero/equality questions for linear recurrences already meet
Skolem-type barriers. The present tools therefore do not justify promoting
that semidecision procedure to a decision theorem.

## Deep exploration: bounded-support classification (2026-08-08)

Six theorem candidates were compared on a five-point scale (reach, novelty,
value).

1. General decidability of geometric synchronized schemes: (2, 5, 5).
   Corollary 3.15 checks any guessed scheme, but no finite bound on fan-out or
   block length is available. The nearest decision literature is the Skolem
   problem for linear recurrences, including Min Sha,
   "Effective results on the Skolem Problem for linear recurrence sequences,"
   J. Number Theory 201 (2019), DOI 10.1016/j.jnt.2018.08.012.
2. Classification for standard linear Pisot systems: (5, 4, 5). Greedy length
   thresholds and Pisot asymptotics force a geometric ratio to equal a power
   of the dominant root. The nearest numeration work is Frougny's finite
   normalization theory and Sadahiro's "Multiple points of tilings associated
   with Pisot numeration systems," DOI 10.1016/j.tcs.2006.02.017; neither
   concerns bounded prime support or MCFLs.
3. Prime support of every geometric ratio is contained in the minimal tail
   constant: (5, 4, 4). This follows from the manuscript's local return
   congruence. Evertse's 1984 S-unit recurrence theorem is the nearest
   arithmetic input, but it does not give this numeration-specific local
   restriction.
4. Remove the valuation-only escape alternative for nonintegral Pisot bases:
   (5, 4, 5). This is the universal negative half of candidate 2. The nearest
   prior work is again Pisot normalization/recognizability, not prime-support
   behavior of MCFL sublanguages.
5. Compute the local Cantor--Bendixson behavior with unbounded bad-prime
   valuations: (5, 3, 3). Broughan, "Adic Topologies for the Rational
   Integers," DOI 10.4153/CJM-2003-030-3, is nearest; the claim would mainly
   sharpen why the manuscript's bounded local valuations are necessary.
6. Extend the quantitative density theorem to bounded-ambiguity automata:
   (2, 2, 3). The deterministic stochastic decomposition does not control
   multiplicities or cancellation. Nearby ambiguity work includes Christian
   Herzog, "Pushdown automata with bounded nondeterminism and bounded
   ambiguity," DOI 10.1016/S0304-3975(96)00267-8, but it supplies no required
   prime-slice asymptotics.

Live API checks used the arXiv Atom API, Crossref, Semantic Scholar, and
zbMATH. Exact arXiv queries combining "multiple context-free" or "Pisot
numeration" with "geometric progression" or "bounded prime support" returned
zero entries. Crossref returned separate Pisot numeration, recurrence
prime-divisor, adic-topology, and ambiguity papers, with no combined result.
Semantic Scholar's broad search endpoint returned HTTP 429, so DOI lookups
were used to confirm Sadahiro (2006), Broughan (2003), and the classical
second-order recurrence prime-divisor record. Exact combined zbMATH searches
returned no entries; broader searches found 120 Pisot-numeration records and
105 multiple-context-free-language records, but no overlap matching the new
classification. These are negative search results, not a priority proof.

Candidates 2--4 were proved together. In a geometric synchronized scheme,
the finite-group block return modulo \(p^{v_p(c)+1}\) excludes every
\(p\mid b\) with \(p\nmid c_0\). For a linear Pisot greedy system, a scheme
of word lengths \(L+Dt\) satisfies
\[
U_{L+Dt-1}\le cb^t<U_{L+Dt},
\]
so \(b=\beta^D\). A nonintegral Pisot number cannot have a positive integral
power: every conjugate would then have modulus \(\beta>1\). Conversely, an
integer root \(B\) supplies the canonical ray \(0^{J+t}1\) of values
\(U_JB^t\). Thus the bounded-outside-support property is decidable on the
standard linear Pisot class and is positive exactly in degree one.

## Tier-up decision audit and refreshed prior art (2026-08-10)

The arXiv Atom API, Crossref, Semantic Scholar, and zbMATH Open APIs were
searched before the new proof audit. Exact arXiv combinations of `multiple
context-free` with `geometric progression` returned no record; searches for
effective linear-recurrence decision problems returned Min Sha's arXiv
1505.07147 and the current Skolem literature. Crossref confirmed the metadata
for Sha (DOI 10.1016/j.jnt.2018.08.012), Tarasov--Vyalyi (DOI
10.1007/978-3-642-20712-9_24), Ouaknine--Worrell (DOI
10.1007/978-3-642-33512-9_3), and Bell--Halava--Harju--Karhumaki--Potapov
(DOI 10.1142/S0218196708004925). Semantic Scholar's DOI lookup for Sha
succeeded and exposed the surrounding orbit/Skolem citation graph; later
broad searches were rate-limited and are not treated as negative evidence.
zbMATH returned the Skolem decision survey and Tarasov--Vyalyi's regular-
language/orbit work, while the exact MCFL/numeration/geometric query returned
no record.

The nearest mechanism remains the pair of classical inputs already used:
Seki--Matsumura--Fujii--Kasami's weak synchronized pumping lemma and
Evertse's Theorem 3 on unbounded prime-ideal support in quotients of a
nondegenerate recurrence. The nearest algorithmic boundary is
Tarasov--Vyalyi's equivalence between an orbit-hitting problem and a regular-
language intersection problem, together with the still-open general Skolem
problem surveyed by Ouaknine--Worrell. Bell et al. prove undecidability for
unrestricted multiplicative matrix equations, but their matrices do not
preserve the companion-form digit action or canonical conditions (U1)--(U3),
so this is not an undecidability result for the manuscript's promise class.

The attempted full decision procedure stops at witness compression. For a
fixed synchronized scheme, DFA acceptance and the identity `N(t)=c b^t` are
decidable. To decide negative instances one needs a computable bound, from
the DFA and minimal tail recurrence, on fan-out and total block length of a
smallest positive witness. Ordinary DFA cycle deletion does not supply it:
two words with the same DFA endpoints can have different affine recurrence
matrices, and replacing one by the other can destroy `N(t+1)=b N(t)`. No
located result provides this bound, and no promise-preserving reduction was
obtained. General decidability, undecidability, and hardness therefore remain
open; only the existing positive semidecision and the linear Pisot decision
theorem are integrated.

## Submission consistency check (2026-08-15)

The article's present priority narrative remains consistent with this audit.
It treats the Seki weak pumping lemma, Broughan's adic classifications,
Evertse's quotient theorem, and the fixed-base Hartmanis--Shank and
Schutzenberger results as prior inputs. The retained claims are limited to the
recurrence-language combination stated with its canonical-presentation
hypotheses, the deleted-prime local rank calculation, the nonunit escape
dichotomy, the geometric-ray characterization, and the stated unit and
Perron-recurrence consequences. The separately submitted finite-state results
remain in Supplementary Information and are compared explicitly with Shen and
Dubbe. No broader priority claim is made for fixed-base prime CF-immunity or
for arbitrary objects called Pisot numeration systems.

\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Context-free coprime-quotient Zeckendorf pumping]
  \label{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}
  Let \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\) be context-free. There is an integer
  \(P_L\ge1\) such that every \(w\in L\) with
  \(\abs w\ge P_L\) and \(\val(w)\ge2\) begins a sequence
  \(w_0=w,w_1,w_2,\ldots\) in \(L\) whose lengths are strictly
  increasing and whose values \(N_i=\val(w_i)\) satisfy
  \[
    N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i,\qquad Q_i>1,\qquad
    Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i},\qquad \gcd(Q_i,N_i)=1.
  \]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Apply Ogden's lemma with every position distinguished. It supplies a
  constant \(P_L\) such that each \(w\in L\) of length at least \(P_L\)
  has a factorisation
  \[
    w=uvxyz,\qquad \abs v+\abs y>0,\qquad
    uv^kxy^kz\in L\quad(k\ge0).
  \]
  The additional marked-position bound in Ogden's lemma is not needed
  here. Starting with \(w_i\in L\), choose such a factorisation and
  let \(h_i\) be a common multiple of the orders of \(M_v\) and \(M_y\)
  in
  \(\operatorname{GL}_3(\ZZ/N_i^2\ZZ)\), omitting an empty block. These
  orders exist by
  \Cref{lem:main-zeckendorf-unimodular-block-action}. Define
  \[
    w_{i+1}:=uv^{\,1+h_i}xy^{\,1+h_i}z.
  \]
  By \Cref{lem:zeckendorf-paired-pumping-congruence},
  \(N_{i+1}\equiv N_i\pmod {N_i^2}\). Hence
  \(N_{i+1}=N_i(1+t_iN_i)\) for an integer \(t_i\).

  Since \(h_i\ge1\) and \(\abs v+\abs y>0\), the new word is longer.
  Both words belong to \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\), and canonical
  Zeckendorf intervals are strictly ordered by length; consequently
  \(N_{i+1}>N_i\), so \(t_i\ge1\). Taking
  \(Q_i=1+t_iN_i\) gives all four assertions. The new word still has
  length at least \(P_L\) and value at least \(2\), so the construction
  iterates.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Zeckendorf CF-immunity package]
  \label{cor:zeckendorf-context-free-immunity-package}
  Every infinite context-free \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\) satisfies
  \[
    \sup_{w\in L}\omega(\val(w))=\infty.
  \]
  Consequently, for each \(K\ge1\), the bounded-distinct-prime language
  \(\mathcal B_K\), the bounded-almost-prime language \(\mathcal A_K\),
  and every infinite finite-prime-support language \(\mathcal Z_S\)
  are CF-immune. In particular, the Zeckendorf prime language
  \(\mathcal P_{\mathcal Z}\) is CF-immune.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  Choose a sufficiently long word in \(L\) and apply
  \Cref{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}. Since
  \(N_j\mid N_i\) for \(j\le i\), while every \(Q_i>1\) is coprime to
  \(N_i\), a prime divisor of \(Q_i\) divides no earlier value. Thus
  \(\omega(N_i)\ge\omega(N_0)+i\). The immunity conclusions follow
  exactly as in the regular cases: \(\omega\le K\) on \(\mathcal B_K\),
  \(\mathcal A_K\subseteq\mathcal B_K\), and
  \(\omega\le\abs S\) on \(\mathcal Z_S\). Finally prime values have
  \(\omega=1\), while Euclid's theorem makes
  \(\mathcal P_{\mathcal Z}\) infinite.
\end{proof}

We next isolate the precise base-independent interface. This is
deliberately narrower than every object called a Pisot or abstract
numeration system in the literature.

  \label{def:canonical-eventually-recurrent-numeration}
  A canonical eventually recurrent numeration system consists of:

Put \(S=\{p:p\text{ is prime and }p\mid a_0\}\). On
\(\NN_{\ge1}\), let \(\tau_S\) be the topology generated by the basis
\[
  \{(n+q\ZZ)\cap\NN_{\ge1}:n\ge1,\ q\ge1,\ \gcd(q,a_0)=1\}.
\]
Its completion is
\[
  \widehat{\ZZ}_{S^c}
    :=\varprojlim_{\gcd(q,a_0)=1}\ZZ/q\ZZ
    \cong\prod_{p\notin S}\ZZ_p.
\]
These are the adic topologies and completions studied by Broughan
\cite{Broughan2003}. His Theorem~2.4 and Corollary~3.1 supply the
\(\mathbb Q\)-space and Cantor-completion classifications used below. The
new assertion here is that every synchronized MCFL orbit satisfies their
no-isolated-point hypothesis, and hence yields the scattered-set
obstruction.

一、编辑建议
建议：现稿不予直接接收；作实质性大修（major revision）。若期刊制度不允许补交完整材料并重构证明，则建议拒稿后重投。
本判断不是因为已经发现主定理的反例。就目前能够审查的二十二页正文而言，核心链条
弱同步泵送⟶模有限群返回⟶删失同余拓扑⟶素因子支撑障碍
在总体上是可信的，几何射线刻画、单位系统整除树和 Perron 子类分类亦具有可发表的数学内容。正文摘要所列贡献与后文定理基本对应，并未出现摘要显著夸大正文的情形。
然而，现版本至少存在一个实质证明缺口、一个投稿材料完整性问题、一个必须深化的优先权问题，以及若干需要精确化的量词和有效性问题。因此我不能建议按现稿接收。
另需明确：本次实际可得附件只有正文 PDF；所谓独立补充材料、Python 脚本、单元测试和存档输出均未随附件提供。 下述对补充材料和机器核验的意见只能根据正文及来稿说明作程序性判断，不能替代对相应文件的正式审查。

二、数学正确性
1. 弱 MCFL 泵引理及固定前缀处理
裁决
Theorem 2.6 所需要的语言论输入，与 Seki–Matsumura–Fujii–Kasami 的弱泵送结论在性质上相符：它只从一个无限有限扇出 MCFL 中产生一个同步可泵族，而不对每个充分长词作断言。正文也正确地区分了这一点与强泵送性质，并引用 Kanazawa 等的反例。
固定长度 J 的前缀 a、对余语言应用弱泵引理、再将后缀动作置于递推稳定区间的处理也是正确方向。矩阵返回时间独立于参数 t，而分解先于模数 q 选定，因此“固定轨道、逐模返回”的量词次序没有错误。
必须修正之处
正文写道，MCFL 对固定词左商闭合且“不增加扇出”，仅笼统指向 “[62, Section 3]”。对于 Theorem 2.6 明确保留原来的 k 并产生 2k 个同步泵块而言，这一扇出保持性不是无关紧要的技术注释。

六、机器核验与可重复性
已存档输出仍记录旧版 5 个系统、917 个样例，而当前脚本声称覆盖 6 个系统、2282 个仿射样例等。这必须在投稿前修正。
这不会直接使数学定理失效，因为作者已明确称脚本为有限一致性检查而非形式证明；但在可重复性意义上，当前归档文件对不上当前脚本，任何“重跑全部通过”的陈述都无法由提交包验证。
消除意见的标准：


用提交版本脚本重新生成输出文件；


输出首部记录：


脚本版本或 Git commit；


脚本 SHA-256；


Python 版本；


完整命令行；


随机种子，若存在随机性；




保证脚本无网络依赖并从仓库根目录可单命令执行；


存档 unittest 的完整输出；


在 README 中列出预期样例计数和失败计数；


CI 或本地重跑若产生不同样例总数，应失败而不是静默覆盖；


删除旧输出，不能把新旧文件同时保留而不解释。


若作者不准备维护这些材料，则应从论文中删除具体运行计数，只保留“提供了辅助有限检查脚本”的非证据性说明。

七、最终结论
我没有发现足以证明主定理错误的反例。特别是：


弱泵送轨道的量词次序正确；


Evertse 的适用范围基本正确；


固定外部素因子预算到固定支撑子递推的转移正确；


内在导集公式正确；


整除树的诱导性质原则上正确；


Perron 整数主根分类原则上正确。


但 Lemma 2.14 的有理性步骤必须重证；缺失 supplement 使 Zeckendorf 部分目前不可正式核查；最新 substitution lemma 和相邻 automaticity 文献必须作实质比较；独立补充结果应从本次投稿拆出；机器输出不一致必须修复。
在完成这些修改后，我认为论文不需要增加更强定理即可达到《Monatshefte für Mathematik》的合理发表门槛。现阶段的恰当决定是：

三、新颖性与优先权
1. 总体判断
在我针对下列组合进行的定向检索中，没有发现一篇先行文献已经给出本文的完整结论：


任意规范最终整递推记数系统；


无限有限扇出 MCFL 子语言；


固定同步泵送轨道；


对所有与尾常数互素的模数返回；


由此推出 prime 与 bounded-Ω 的 MCF-immunity；


再以最小尾递推刻画 bounded external prime support 的几何同步射线。


因此，核心综合定理具有可信的新颖性。但这仍是负面检索结论，不是优先权证明；投稿中不应使用 “first” 或 “no previous result” 一类绝对措辞。

2. 必须正面比较 Duncan–Elder–Frenkel–Liu
2026 年版本的 Duncan–Elder–Frenkel–Liu 给出了 MCFL 的 substitution lemma，其特点正是对所有充分长词施加替换型必要条件，而不是仅抽取一个弱泵送轨道。arXiv 稿件参考文献已经列入该文，但正文只把它隐含在“强泵引理失败”的周边叙述中。
这是当前优先权叙事中最明显的不足。作者必须说明：


substitution lemma 是否可以直接推出本文任何 immunity 结论；


若不能，障碍是否在于替换块不保持本文所需的同一参数同步、固定仿射矩阵幂或全模返回；


本文为何仍选择 1991 年弱泵引理，而不是 2026 年更强的语言论工具；


substitution lemma 是否能缩短 Theorem 2.2 的 CFL 专门论证，或增强任意 MCFL 的结论。


不能仅把该文列入参考文献而不作内容比较。若该工具能够直接给出某项 immunity，本稿相应结果的原创性叙述必须调整；若不能，应把“不相容之处”写成精确的技术差异，而非一般性表态。

3. Evertse–Schur 部分不能作为独立原创定理宣传
从非退化多根递推的项商素理想支撑无界，推导固定 S-支撑递推最终只能在算术子序列上具有单根形式，是 Evertse 定理的标准用途之一。较新的 S-part 文献也继续研究递推项的有限素数支撑和大小问题。IRMA Strasbourg
因此 Lemma 2.14 即使正文给出了完整推导，其原创性应定位为：

为本文语言论接口整理并证明一个所需的算术刚性引理。

不应把“固定素数支撑递推含纯几何算术子序列”列作与 Theorem 2.15 同等级的独立新发现。真正的新内容是：


如何从 MCFL 的一条模返回轨道取得固定支撑子递推；


如何把算术子序列重新编码为同步几何语言；


以及由此得到最小尾递推下的等价刻画。


正文第 1.1 节已称 Evertse 为 classical input，这是正确方向，但摘要中的 “we prove an inverse theorem” 应确保读者不会把其中的全部算术刚性都理解为原创。

4. Broughan 重叠应进一步降调
Theorem 2.8(i)–(ii) 所使用的 Q/Cantor 分类实质上是 Broughan 论文的主要结构结论，而不是本文产生的新拓扑现象。剑桥大学出版社+1
因此建议把贡献拆成：


既有结论： adic 空间及其完成的拓扑分类；


本文结论： 弱 MCFL 轨道必落入该分类中的无孤立情形；


本文局部精化： XK,E​ 的删失素数、不同素因子数和内在导集公式。


若继续使用 “local-adic topological fingerprint” 作为主要新颖性标题，会使审稿人认为作者在重新包装 Broughan。

5. 固定进制及 REG 层面的优先权
正文正确引用 Hartmanis–Shank 和 Schützenberger，并未把固定进制 CF-immunity 作为新结果。这个界线是准确的。
但需要进一步明确：普通整数基下由 Theorem 2.10 得到的是 MCF-immunity，而经典 1968 结果只给出 CF 层面的障碍。Example 2.23 中 “recovering the classical conclusion” 容易被理解为整个 MCF 结论只是重述经典结果。 建议改为：

This strengthens the classical fixed-base CF-immunity conclusion to MCF-immunity.

补充材料若包含固定 DFA 的素数切片误差下界，还必须逐项比较 Shen 2022 和 Dubbe 2025。Shen 已系统强化“素数自动机不存在”的有限自动机障碍，而 Dubbe 2025 的论文题目和内容直接聚焦素数集合的 automaticity。科学直通车+1 在未见补充材料定理的情况下，我不能确认其定量下界是否已有覆盖。这一比较是补充材料能够保留的前提。

四、是否达到《Monatshefte für Mathematik》的发表门槛
1. 学术层级
若上述证明缺口得到修复、优先权叙事得到重写，并删除与主链无关的材料，本稿现有定理增量足以构成《Monatshefte für Mathematik》层级的可发表论文。
理由不在单独的 prime MCF-immunity，而在于以下组合：


对一般最终整递推规范记数系统建立固定的 MCFL 局部返回轨道；


给出非单位系统的素支撑/赋值二择一；


在最小尾递推下得到 bounded external support 与几何同步射线的等价；


给出正例半判定和精确算法边界；


单位情形构造诱导全分支整除树；


对严格主导 Perron 贪心基作出整数主根分类。


这组结果具有统一机制，不是彼此无关的小推论。目标期刊的官方范围是广义纯数学，并要求应用方向文章具有实质数学内容；本稿的数论、拓扑和形式语言交叉内容在范围上没有问题。Springer
2. 为什么现稿仍未达到接收标准
主要不足不是缺少更强定理，而是：


核心算术引理存在需要实质补写的论证；


一个正式正文定理依赖未提供的 supplement；


经典拓扑部分在贡献叙述中所占权重过高；


最新 MCFL substitution lemma 尚未进行实质比较；


有效性结论未清楚标为承诺问题；


补充材料与正文主链的主题跨度使文章显得像两个项目的汇编。


因此不应要求作者再解决新的开放问题，但必须要求其把现有结果提交成一个自足、优先权边界清楚且证明完整的论文。
3. 更匹配的备选期刊
若作者希望保留完整的固定 DFA 密度、素数切片、Zeckendorf 正则增长和机器验证材料，而不缩减为数学主链，则 Theoretical Computer Science 会比《Monatshefte》更自然。该刊明确以数学化、抽象的计算理论为范围，形式语言、自动机和可判定性材料可以与 MCFL 主体并列，而不必证明所有附加结果都服务于一个纯数学中心命题。科学直通车
但若按下述建议集中正文，我认为没有必要仅因层级不足而主动降投。
